\displaystyle-\mskip-19.5mu\int_{B^{(\lambda)}_{r}(x_{o})}\frac{|u(t)-a|^{2}}{\varrho^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(4.2)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\bigg[\frac{|u-a|^{p}}{\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}p}(\varrho-r)^{p}}+\frac{|u-a|^{2}}{\varrho^{2}-r^{2}}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{p}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{p^{\prime}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

4.2.
Gluing lemma

We record a radius–selection estimate controlling the temporal oscillation of spatial means. Writing the time slice-mean

\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{x_{o};r}(t):=-\mskip-19.5mu\int_{B^{(\lambda)}_{r}(x_{o})}u(x,t)\,\mathrm{d}x,

the lemma shows that for a suitable
\hat{\varrho}\in[\frac{1}{2}\varrho,\varrho)
the variation of
\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{x_{o};\hat{\varrho}}(t)
is bounded by the space–time integral of the natural flux terms.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
p>1
. There exists a constant
C=C(N,C_{1})>0
with the following property. If
u
is a weak solution to
(
1.1
) in
E_{T}
in the sense of Definition
2.5
, satisfying (
1.2
), and if
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subseteq E_{T}
, then there exists
\hat{\varrho}\in[\tfrac{\varrho}{2},\varrho)
such that for all
t_{1},t_{2}\in\Lambda_{\varrho}(t_{o})
,

\big|\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{x_{o};\hat{\varrho}}(t_{2})-\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{x_{o};\hat{\varrho}}(t_{1})\big|\leq C\lambda^{\frac{2-p}{2}}\varrho\,\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\!\Big[|Du|^{p-1}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{p-1}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Proof..

It is a straightforward adaptation of the argument used in
[
15
, Lemma 3.1]
. We refrain from going into further details.
∎

4.3.
Parabolic Sobolev–Poincaré-type inequalities

A parabolic Poincaré-type inequality on space–time cylinders is recorded next. Combining the slice-wise Poincaré estimate with the radius–selection bound from Lemma
4.2
controls the deviation from the space–time mean by the spatial gradient and
by the temporal oscillation encoded in the flux terms.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
p>1
. There exists a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{1})>0
with the following property. If
u
is a weak solution to (
1.1
) in
E_{T}
in the sense of Definition
2.5
, satisfying (
1.2
), and if
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\equiv Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subseteq E_{T}
, then for any
\sigma\in[1,p]
one has the Poincaré-type inequality

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\sigma}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\bigg[\lambda^{2-p}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|Du|^{p-1}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{p-1}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\sigma}.

Proof..

Throughout the proof we suppress the center
z_{o}
from the notation. Let
\hat{\varrho}\in[\frac{1}{2}\varrho,\varrho)
be the radius provided by Lemma
4.2
; by the quasi–minimality Lemma
2.3
, we get

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}

\displaystyle\frac{\big|u-(u)^{(\lambda)}_{\varrho}\big|^{\sigma}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq 2^{\sigma}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}\big|^{\sigma}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq 8[{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}+{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}],

where

{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}:=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}(t)\big|^{\sigma}}{\varrho^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,\qquad{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}:=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}\!\int_{\Lambda_{\varrho}}\frac{\big|\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}(t)-(u)^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}\big|^{\sigma}}{\varrho^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}t,

and moreover,

\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}(t):=-\mskip-19.5mu\int_{B^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}}u(x,t)\,\mathrm{d}x\quad\mbox{and}\quad(u)^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}:=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\hat{\varrho}}^{(\lambda)}}u\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Next, we estimate
{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}
and
{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}
. Since
\hat{\varrho}\in[\frac{1}{2}\varrho,\varrho)
, Lemma
2.3
and, subsequently, Poincaré’s inequality on the ball
B_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
(applied slice-wise) yield

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u-\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{\varrho}(t)|^{\sigma}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

with
C=C(N,p)
. The dependence of the constant in Poincaré’s inequality on
\sigma\in[1,p]
is continuous and can be absorbed into
C
.
As for
{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}
, by Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\sigma}}\sup_{t,\tau\in\Lambda_{\varrho}}\big|\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}(t)-\langle u\rangle^{(\lambda)}_{\hat{\varrho}}(\tau)\big|^{\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\lambda^{2-p}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|Du|^{p-1}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{p-1}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\sigma},

with
C=C(N,C_{1})
. Combining the estimates for
{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}
and
{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}
yields the claim.
∎

The next lemma records a Poincaré-type inequality on intrinsic cylinders.
The estimate follows from Lemma
4.3
together with Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities. The parameter
\varepsilon
balances the mean-oscillation term against the energy and source terms.

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
1<p<2
. There exists a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{1})>0
with the following property. If
u
is a weak solution to (
1.1
) in
E_{T}
in the sense of Definition
2.5
, satisfying (
1.2
), and if
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subseteq E_{T}
, then for any
\sigma\in[p-1,p]
and any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\sigma}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\lambda^{\sigma}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{2-p}{p-1}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\Big[|Du|^{\sigma}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{\sigma}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{\frac{\sigma}{p-1}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where we recall that

(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\lambda)}:=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Proof..

We suppress
z_{o}
in the notation; apply Lemma
4.3

and Hölder’s inequality to get

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\sigma}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\sigma}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\,\,\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\lambda^{\sigma(2-p)}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|Du|^{p-1}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{p-1}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\sigma}

\displaystyle\,\,\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\lambda^{\sigma(2-p)}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|Du|^{\sigma}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{\sigma}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{\frac{\sigma}{p-1}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{p-1}.

Apply Young’s inequality with exponents
\frac{1}{2-p}
and
\frac{1}{p-1}
to the second term on the right-hand side and conclude the proof.
∎

4.4.
Estimates on (sub-)intrinsic cylinders

Fix
{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}>2
such that (
1.3
) holds.
Throughout this subsection, a cylinder
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subseteq E_{T}
is said to satisfy a
sub-intrinsic coupling
if, for some
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\geq 1
,

(4.3)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(2\varrho)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\lambda^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

Likewise,
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
is called
super-intrinsic
if, for some
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\geq 1
,

(4.4)

\displaystyle\lambda^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\varrho^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

The next result provides a quantitative
L^{\infty}
-estimate on these cylinders.

Corollary 4.5
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
, for any
\tau\in(0,1)
and any cylinder
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subseteq E_{T}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\tau\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|u|

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{\lambda^{(p-2)\frac{N}{p}}}{(1-\tau)^{N+p}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\frac{|u|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{pq}}+\lambda\Bigg],

where
\bm{\lambda}_{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}=N(p-2)+p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
and
C
depends only on the structural data.

Proof..

Set
z_{o}=0
; apply Theorem
1.1
with
(\varrho,\theta)
replaced by
\big(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho,\ \varrho^{2}\big)
to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\tau\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|u|

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\frac{1}{(1-\tau)^{N+p}}\bigg(\frac{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{p}}{\varrho^{2}}\bigg)^{\frac{N}{p}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|u|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\bigg[\bigg(\frac{\varrho^{2}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{p}}\bigg)^{q-\frac{N}{p}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{2q}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\bigg[\bigg(\frac{\varrho^{2}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{p}}\bigg)^{p^{\prime}(q-\frac{N}{p})}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{p}{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{p^{\prime}\bm{\lambda}_{q}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\bigg[\frac{\varrho^{2}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{p}}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2-p}}

\displaystyle=C\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\Bigg[\bigg[\frac{\lambda^{(p-2)\frac{N}{p}}}{(1-\tau)^{N+p}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\lambda^{1-pq\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{2q}}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{2q}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\lambda^{1-pq\frac{p}{p^{\prime}\bm{\lambda}_{q}}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{p^{\prime}\bm{\lambda}_{q}}}+\lambda\Bigg].

Applying Young’s inequality to the second term with conjugate exponents
\big(1-pq\,\tfrac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{2q}}\big)^{-1}
and
\big(pq\,\tfrac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{2q}}\big)^{-1}
, and to the third term with
\big(1-pq\,\tfrac{p}{p^{\prime}\bm{\lambda}_{q}}\big)^{-1}
and
\big(pq\,\tfrac{p}{p^{\prime}\bm{\lambda}_{q}}\big)^{-1}
, one arrives at the desired estimate.
∎

The
u
-dependence of
{\bm{\mathsf{A}}}
is not allowed in the following result as we have to guarantee that
u+const.
is also a solution.

Lemma 4.6

(Intrinsic
L^{\infty}

estimate on sub-intrinsic cylinders)
.

Let
p\in\bigl(1,\tfrac{2N}{N+2}\bigr]
and
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\geq 1
. If
u
is a weak solution to (
1.1
) in
E_{T}
in the sense of Definition
2.5
, satisfying (
1.2
), and if
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subseteq E_{T}
is a parabolic cylinder fulfilling (
4.3
),
then

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|}{\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho}\leq C\,\lambda+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{pq}},

with a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}},{\bm{\mathsf{K}}})
,
where

(u)_{z_{o};2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}:=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}u\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Proof..

Omit
z_{o}
from the notation.
First observe that
u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
is again a weak solution to (
1.1
). Applying Corollary
4.5
to
u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
on
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
and taking
\tau=1/2
yield

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|}{\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\lambda^{(p-2)\frac{N}{p}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(2\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{pq}}+C\lambda,

where
\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}=N(p-2)+p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
.
In view of the sub-intrinsic condition (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{(p-2)\frac{N}{p}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(2\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\lambda^{(p-2)\frac{N}{p}-\frac{p-2}{2}{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\lambda^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}={\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\lambda^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{p}}.

Consequently,

\Bigg[\lambda^{(p-2)\frac{N}{p}}\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{(2\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}^{\frac{p}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\lambda,

so that the conclusion follows.
∎

Assuming both the sub-intrinsic coupling (
4.3
) and the super-intrinsic bound (
4.4
), we arrive at the following estimate.

Lemma 4.7

(intrinsic scale bound)
.

Let
p\in\bigl(1,\tfrac{2N}{N+2}\bigr]
and
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\geq 1
. There exists a constant

C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}},{\bm{\mathsf{K}}})>0
with the following property. If
u
is a weak
solution to (
1.1
) in
E_{T}
in the sense of
Definition
2.5
, satisfying (
1.2
), and if

Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subseteq E_{T}
is a parabolic cylinder fulfilling
(
4.3
) and (
4.4
), then

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}.

The cylinders
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
are defined in the intrinsic geometry of the parabolic
p
-Laplacian, with spatial radius
\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho
and time height
\varrho^{2}
; the parameter
\lambda
encodes the local scale of oscillation/degeneracy. The sub-intrinsic and super-intrinsic conditions calibrate this scale by tying
\lambda
to the
L^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
-oscillation of
u
on
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
and
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
. Then, the lemma shows that this intrinsic scale is bounded – quantitatively controlled – by the local energy and the source terms.
Thus, the result is a scale bound for
\lambda
: it prevents an uncalibrated choice of intrinsic cylinders, converts geometric coupling into energetic control, and furnishes the normalization needed for subsequent local estimates.

Proof..

Throughout the proof, the reference point
z_{o}
is suppressed. From the super-intrinsic property (
4.4
) it follows that

\displaystyle\lambda^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\Bigg[\sup_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)}\Bigg]^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{p}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

To estimate the sup-term, observe that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\big|u-(u)_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\big|u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|+\big|(u)_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\big|u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|+\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\big|u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\big|u-(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|.

Then, Lemma
4.6
yields

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|}{\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho}\leq C\lambda+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{pq}}.

Substituting the preceding bound and applying the Poincaré inequality from Corollary
4.4
together with Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\lambda+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{pq}}\Bigg]^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\bigg[\varepsilon\lambda^{p}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\frac{2-p}{p-1}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|Du|^{p}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{p}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{p^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\Bigg[\lambda^{p}+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}{p}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Bigg[\lambda^{p}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}\bigg\{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\varepsilon+C_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{p}}

for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. Choosing
\varepsilon>0
so small that
C\,\varepsilon\leq\tfrac{1}{2}
, the first term on the right can be absorbed into the left–hand side, and the previous estimate yields

\lambda^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\leq C\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{p}}.

Raising both sides to the power
p/{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
gives the desired bound.
∎

4.5.
Reverse Hölder-type inequality

Under the sub- and super-intrinsic assumptions (
4.5
) – (
4.6
), the intrinsic parameter
\lambda
is calibrated by the local energy and the source terms (cf. Lemma
4.7
). In combination with the Caccioppoli estimate and the parabolic Poincaré inequality on intrinsic cylinders (Corollary
4.4
), this calibration prevents loss of scale and yields a self-improvement of integrability for
|Du|
in the Gehring sense. The next proposition records the resulting reverse Hölder inequality on
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
, with constants depending only on the structural quantities and the coupling parameter
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}
.

Proposition 4.8

(Reverse Hölder on intrinsic cylinders)
.

Let
p>1
and
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\geq 1
. There exists a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}},{\bm{\mathsf{K}}})>0
with the following property. If
u
is a weak solution to (
1.1
) in
E_{T}
in the sense of Definition
2.5
, satisfying (
1.2
), and if
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subseteq E_{T}
is a parabolic cylinder on which both

(4.5)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(2\varrho)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\lambda^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

and

(4.6)

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg]

hold, then the reverse Hölder inequality

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\theta}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}},

is valid, where
\theta:=\frac{Np}{N+2}
.

Proof..

Throughout the proof, the reference point
z_{o}
is suppressed from the notation. Set
a:=(u)_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
and define

\displaystyle\phi(r)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{r}}-\mskip-19.5mu\int_{B_{r}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u(t)-a|^{2}}{r^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

for
\varrho\leq r\leq 2\varrho
. Applying Lemma
4.1
on nested cylinders
Q_{r}^{(\lambda)}\subset Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}
for
\varrho\leq r<s\leq 2\varrho
gives the energy inequality

\displaystyle\phi(r)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\bigg[\Big(\frac{s}{s-r}\Big)^{p}\frac{|u-a|^{p}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s)^{p}}+\Big(\frac{s}{s-r}\Big)^{2}\frac{|u-a|^{2}}{s^{2}}\bigg]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{p}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{p^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Since
p<2<{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
, interpolate between
L^{p}
and
L^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
to control the
L^{2}
-term on the right-hand side. Using (
4.5
) and then Young’s inequality with conjugate exponents
\tfrac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}{2-p}
and
\tfrac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}

\displaystyle\frac{|u-a|^{2}}{s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u-a|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{s^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2-p}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u-a|^{p}}{s^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-2}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{\frac{p(p-2)({\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-2)}{2({\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p)}}\bigg[\underbrace{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u-a|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(2\varrho)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}_{\leq{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\lambda^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}}\bigg]^{\frac{2-p}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u-a|^{p}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s)^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-2}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}}

\displaystyle=C\lambda^{\frac{p(2-p)}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u-a|^{p}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s)^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-2}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-p}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\varepsilon}\lambda^{p}+\frac{C}{\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{2-p}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-2}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u-a|^{p}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s)^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

for any
\tilde{\varepsilon}\in(0,1]
. Substituting the preceding interpolation bound into the energy inequality gives, for
\varrho\leq r<s\leq 2\varrho
,

\displaystyle\phi(r)

\displaystyle\leq C{\bm{\mathsf{R}}}_{r,s}^{2}\lambda^{p}\tilde{\varepsilon}+\frac{C\,{\bm{\mathsf{R}}}_{r,s}^{p}}{\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{2-p}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-2}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u-a|^{p}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s)^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}},

where
{\bm{\mathsf{R}}}_{r,s}:=\frac{\varrho}{s-r}
and

{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}:=\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Here
{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}
arises from the source terms after an application of Hölder’s inequality in the energy estimate.
Applying the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality from Lemma
2.4
on
Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}
(with
|u-a|/(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s)
) yields

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}

\displaystyle\frac{|u-a|^{p}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s)^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\lambda^{2-p}\sup_{t\in\Lambda_{s}}\underbrace{-\mskip-19.5mu\int_{B_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\frac{|u(t)-a|^{2}}{s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{\leq\phi(s)}\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{N+2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}}\bigg[|Du|^{\theta}+\frac{|u-a|^{\theta}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s)^{\theta}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\big[\lambda^{2-p}\phi(s)\big]^{\frac{p}{N+2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\bigg[|Du|^{\theta}+\frac{|u-a|^{\theta}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\theta}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

since
Q_{s}^{(\lambda)}\subset Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
and enlarging the domain of integration only increases the integral.
Applying Young’s inequality with conjugate exponents
\frac{N+2}{p}
and
\frac{N+2}{N+2-p}
to the factor
\phi^{\frac{p}{N+2}}

in the last bound, we obtain

\displaystyle\phi(r)

\displaystyle\leq C{\bm{\mathsf{R}}}_{r,s}^{2}\lambda^{p}\tilde{\varepsilon}+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+\phi^{\frac{p}{N+2}}(s)\cdot\frac{C\,{\bm{\mathsf{R}}}_{r,s}^{p}\lambda^{\frac{p(2-p)}{N+2}}}{\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\frac{2-p}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}-2}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\bigg[|Du|^{\theta}+\frac{|u-a|^{\theta}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\theta}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\phi(s)+C{\bm{\mathsf{R}}}_{r,s}^{2}\lambda^{p}\tilde{\varepsilon}+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C_{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{\bm{\mathsf{R}}}_{r,s}^{\frac{Np}{N-\theta}}\lambda^{\frac{p(2-p)}{N+2-p}}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\bigg[|Du|^{\theta}+\frac{|u-a|^{\theta}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\theta}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{N+2-p}}.

Applying the iteration lemma (Lemma
2.1
) to reabsorb the term
\tfrac{1}{2}\phi(s)
yields

\phi(\varrho)\leq C\lambda^{p}\tilde{\varepsilon}+C_{\tilde{\varepsilon}}\lambda^{\frac{p(2-p)}{N+2-p}}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\bigg[|Du|^{\theta}+\frac{|u-a|^{\theta}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\theta}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{N+2}{N+2-p}}+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}.

A further application of Young’s inequality with conjugate exponents

\frac{N+2-p}{2-p}
and
\frac{N+2-p}{N}
gives

\phi(\varrho)\leq C\lambda^{p}\tilde{\varepsilon}+C_{\tilde{\varepsilon}}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\bigg[|Du|^{\theta}+\frac{|u-a|^{\theta}}{(\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho)^{\theta}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{\theta}}+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}},

where
\tilde{\varepsilon}\in(0,1]
.
Choose
\tilde{\varepsilon}=(4C{\bm{\mathsf{K}}})^{-1}
, where
C
is the constant in front of
\lambda^{p}
in the preceding bound and
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}
is the constant from (
4.6
). Applying Corollary
4.4
on
Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}
with
\sigma=\theta\in[p-1,p]
to
w=u-a
, and using Hölder’s inequality, we infer that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\phi(\varrho)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda^{p}}{4{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}}+C\Bigg[\varepsilon\lambda^{\theta}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\frac{2-p}{p-1}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|Du|^{\theta}+|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{\theta}+\big|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{\frac{\theta}{p-1}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{\theta}}+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}

\displaystyle\leq\big[\tfrac{1}{4{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}}+C\varepsilon\big]\lambda^{p}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{2-p}{p-1}\cdot\frac{p}{\theta}}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\theta}}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\frac{2-p}{p-1}\cdot\frac{p}{\theta}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}.

Choosing
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{4C{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}}
in the preceding bound gives

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq\phi(\varrho)\leq\frac{\lambda^{p}}{2{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}}\,+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\theta}}+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}.

By the super–intrinsic assumption (
4.6
),

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{p}}{2{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\tfrac{1}{2}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Inserting this and absorbing
\tfrac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t
into the left–hand side yields the claimed reverse Hölder inequality.
∎

5.
Higher integrability

To set up the higher integrability scheme, fix a standard cylinder

Q_{8R}(y_{o},\tau_{o})\equiv B_{8R}(y_{o})\times\big(\tau_{o}-(8R)^{2},\tau_{o}+(8R)^{2}\big)\subseteq E_{T}.

Throughout this section the center
(y_{o},\tau_{o})
is suppressed from the notation; write
Q_{\varrho}:=Q_{\varrho}(y_{o},\tau_{o})
for
\varrho\in(0,8R]
. Choose
{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}>2
so that (
1.3
) is satisfied
and introduce a parameter
\lambda_{o}\geq 1
(to be fixed below) satisfying

(5.1)

\lambda_{o}\geq 1+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}\frac{|u-(u)_{4R}|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{(4R)^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}.

For
z_{o}\equiv(x_{o},t_{o})\in Q_{2R}
,
\varrho\in(0,R]
, and
\lambda\geq 1
, consider the cylinders
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
as in (
4.1
). Since the spatial radius is
\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho
and
p\leq 2
, these cylinders are monotone in
\lambda
:

1\leq\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda_{2})}(z_{o})\subset Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda_{1})}(z_{o}).

Moreover,

B^{(\lambda)}_{\varrho}(x_{o})\subset B_{3R}(y_{o})\subset B_{4R}(y_{o}),\qquad\Lambda_{\varrho}(t_{o})\subset\big(\tau_{o}-(4R)^{2},\tau_{o}+(4R)^{2}\big);

hence,
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})\subset Q_{4R}
for all
\lambda\geq 1
.

5.1.
Construction of an intrinsic, non-uniform cylinder system

The following construction of intrinsic cylinders has its origin in
[
9
,
20
]
.
Lemma
5.1
records the basic properties of the scale parameter
\varrho\mapsto\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}
attached to a fixed center
z_{o}\in Q_{R}
and depending on the radius
\varrho
. It is well defined for
\varrho\in(0,R]
, continuous, and non-increasing; it calibrates the intrinsic geometry of
u
at radius
\varrho
, is uniformly controlled from below by the initial level
\lambda_{o}
, and exhibits the correct growth under changes of scale. In particular, the associated cylinders are nested, satisfy a sub-intrinsic oscillation bound,
and satisfy a quantitative dichotomy: either they become intrinsic at some larger radius

\widetilde{\varrho}\in[\varrho,R/2]
or a global bound for
\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}
holds.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\lambda_{o}\geq 1
,
z_{o}\in Q_{R}
, and
u\in L^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(Q_{2R};\mathbb{R}^{k})
. There exists a function
(0,R]\ni\varrho\mapsto\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}\in[\lambda_{o},\infty)
with the following properties:

a)

The function
\varrho\mapsto\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}
is continuous and non-increasing.

b)

For every
s\in(\varrho,R]
,

\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}\leq 4^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{s}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda_{z_{o};s},

and, moreover,

\lambda_{z_{o};R}\leq 4^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda_{o}.

c)

The associated scaled cylinders are nested:

Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho})}(z_{o})\subset Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};s})}(z_{o})\;\subseteq\;Q_{2R}\qquad\text{whenever }\,0<\varrho<s\leq R.

d)

Sub–intrinsic control holds on each scale:

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};s}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{s^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq 2^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\quad\text{for every }s\in[\varrho,R].

e)

Either

\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}\leq 4^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+3{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\lambda_{o}

or there exists
\widetilde{\varrho}\in\big[\varrho,\tfrac{R}{2}\big]
such that

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};\widetilde{\varrho}})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\widetilde{\varrho}^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t=\lambda_{z_{o};\widetilde{\varrho}}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

Proof..

The proof is divided into several steps.

Step 1. Definition of
\widetilde{\lambda}_{z_{o};\varrho}
.

For
z_{o}\in Q_{2R}
,
\varrho\in(0,R]
, and
\lambda\geq 1
, consider the cylinders
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda)}(z_{o})
from (
4.1
). Define

\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}\equiv\widetilde{\lambda}_{z_{o};\varrho}:=\inf\bigg\{\lambda\in[\lambda_{o},\infty):\frac{1}{|Q_{\varrho}|}\iint_{Q^{(\lambda)}_{\varrho}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\varrho^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq\lambda^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\bigg\}.

This quantity is well defined: the set over which the infimum is taken is nonempty, since as
\lambda\to\infty
the spatial radius
\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho
tends to
0
(since
p\leq 2
), hence, the left–hand side tends to
0
by the absolute continuity of the integral, whereas the right–hand side grows like
\lambda^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}
. The choice of the exponent is natural: the condition is equivalent to

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q^{(\lambda)}_{\varrho}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\lambda)}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\varrho^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq\lambda^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}},

which matches the sub/super–intrinsic couplings used in §§
4.4
and
4.5
.
As an immediate consequence of the definition of

\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}
, we obtain the following dichotomy: either

(5.2)

\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}=\lambda_{o}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\varrho^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=\lambda_{o}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

or

(5.3)

\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}>\lambda_{o}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\varrho^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

Also in the case
\varrho=R
we have
\widetilde{\lambda}_{R}\geq\lambda_{o}\geq 1
. Moreover, if
\widetilde{\lambda}_{R}>\lambda_{o}
, then by (
5.3
), Lemma
2.3
, the inclusion
Q_{R}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{R})}(z_{o})\subset Q_{4R}
, and (
5.1
), we find

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{R}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q_{R}|}\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{R})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};R}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{R})}\big|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{R^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{|Q_{R}|}\iint_{Q_{R}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{R})}(z_{o})}\frac{|u-(u)_{4R}|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{R^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{|Q_{R}|}\iint_{Q_{4R}}\frac{|u-(u)_{4R}|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{(4R)^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 4^{N+2+2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

Therefore,

(5.4)

\widetilde{\lambda}_{R}\leq 4^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda_{o}.

Step 2. Continuity of
(0,R]\ni\varrho\mapsto\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}
.

Our next goal is to show that the map
(0,R]\ni\varrho\mapsto\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}
is continuous. Fix
\varrho\in(0,R]
and
\varepsilon>0
, and set
\lambda_{+}:=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}+\varepsilon
. We claim that there exists
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,\varrho)>0
such that for all
r\in(0,R]
with
|r-\varrho|<\delta
,

\frac{1}{|Q_{r}|}\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda_{+})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};r}^{(\lambda_{+})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{r^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t<\lambda_{+}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

Indeed, for
r=\varrho
this is immediate from the definition of
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}
, since
\lambda_{+}>\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}
. By the absolute continuity of the integral and the continuity of the involved averages with respect to the radius, the strict inequality persists for
|r-\varrho|
sufficiently small. By the definition of
\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}
it follows that
\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}<\lambda_{+}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}+\varepsilon
whenever
|r-\varrho|<\delta
. In particular,

\limsup_{r\to\varrho}\,\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}\;\leq\;\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}.

For the reverse inequality, set
\lambda_{-}:=\max\{\lambda_{o},\,\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}-\varepsilon\}
. If
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}=\lambda_{o}
, then
\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}\geq\lambda_{o}\geq\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}-\varepsilon
for all
r
, and there is nothing to prove. If
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}>\lambda_{o}
, then, by the defining equality at the infimum,

\frac{1}{|Q_{\varrho}|}\iint_{Q_{\varrho}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\varrho}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\varrho^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}>\lambda_{-}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

By continuity in the radius (again using absolute continuity), there exists
\delta^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}(\varepsilon,\varrho)>0
such that for all
r
with
|r-\varrho|<\delta^{\prime}
,

\frac{1}{|Q_{r}|}\iint_{Q_{r}^{(\lambda_{-})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};r}^{(\lambda_{-})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{r^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t>\lambda_{-}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

Hence, by the definition of
\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}
, one must have
\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}\geq\lambda_{-}\geq\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}-\varepsilon
for
|r-\varrho|<\delta^{\prime}
. Therefore,

\liminf_{r\to\varrho}\,\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}\;\geq\;\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}-\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we conclude that
\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}\to\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}
as
r\to\varrho
, i.e., the map
\varrho\mapsto\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}
is continuous on
(0,R]
.

Step 3. Definition of
\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}
.

Since the map
(0,R]\ni\varrho\mapsto\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho}
need not be decreasing, introduce its decreasing envelope

\lambda_{\varrho}\equiv\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}:=\max_{r\in[\varrho,R]}\widetilde{\lambda}_{z_{o};r}\,.

By construction,
\lambda_{\varrho}
is monotone with respect to
\varrho
: if
\varrho_{1}<\varrho_{2}
, then
[\varrho_{2},R]\subset[\varrho_{1},R]
and hence
\lambda_{\varrho_{2}}\leq\lambda_{\varrho_{1}}
. Moreover, since
\widetilde{\lambda}_{z_{o};r}
is continuous on every compact interval and maxima are attained, the map
\varrho\mapsto\lambda_{\varrho}=\max_{r\in[\varrho,R]}\widetilde{\lambda}_{r;z_{o}}
is continuous on
(0,R]
. In particular, property a) holds. Finally,
\lambda_{\varrho}\geq\widetilde{\lambda}_{\varrho,z_{o}}\geq\lambda_{o}\geq 1
, so property c) is also satisfied.

Step 4. Sub-intrinsic property.

In general, the cylinders
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda_{\varrho})}(z_{o})
cannot
be expected to be intrinsic in the sense of (
5.3
).
However, we can show that the cylinders
Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{\varrho})}(z_{o})

are sub-intrinsic for all radii
s\in[\varrho,R]
as stated in d).
For the proof of this property, we use Lemma
2.3
, the chain of inequalities

\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\leq\lambda_{s}\leq\lambda_{\varrho}
, which implies

Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{\varrho})}(z_{o})\subseteq Q_{s}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}(z_{o})
, and the fact that the latter
cylinder is sub-intrinsic, i.e. it satisfies either (
5.2
) or (
5.3
). Subsequently, we recall that
\frac{pr}{2}-\frac{N(2-p)}{2}=\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{r}}{2}>0
and again use
\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\leq\lambda_{\varrho}
.
In this way, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{\varrho})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};s}^{(\lambda_{\varrho})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{s^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{\varrho})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};s}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{s^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\Big(\frac{\lambda_{\varrho}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}\Big)^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};s}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{s})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{s^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\Big(\frac{\lambda_{\varrho}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}}\Big)^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\lambda_{\varrho}^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}\,\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}-\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\lambda_{\varrho}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}},

which is exactly assertion d).

Step 5. Upper bound for
\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho}
.

Define

\widetilde{\varrho}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}R,&\quad\mbox{if $\lambda_{\varrho}=\lambda_{o}$,}\\[5.0pt]
\min\big\{s\in[\varrho,R]:\lambda_{s}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}\big\},&\quad\mbox{if $\lambda_{\varrho}>\lambda_{o}$.}\end{array}\right.

By construction,
\lambda_{s}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}}
for every
s\in[\varrho,\widetilde{\varrho}]
, in particular
\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}}=\lambda_{\varrho}
. The aim is to prove

(5.5)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\varrho}\leq 4^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{s}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\lambda_{s}\quad\mbox{for any $s\in(\varrho,R]$.}

If
\lambda_{\varrho}=\lambda_{o}
, the bound is immediate since
\lambda_{s}\geq\lambda_{o}
. If
s\in(\varrho,\widetilde{\varrho}]
, then
\lambda_{s}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}}=\lambda_{\varrho}
and the inequality holds as well. It remains to consider the case
\lambda_{\varrho}>\lambda_{o}
and
s\in(\widetilde{\varrho},R]
.
Fix such an
s
.
Recall that
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}}=\lambda_{\varrho}>\lambda_{o}
. Hence, thanks to (
5.3
) applied at
\widetilde{\varrho}
, and subsequently using Lemma
2.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{\varrho}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}|}\iint_{Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\widetilde{\varrho}^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{|Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}|}\iint_{Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};s}^{(\lambda_{s})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\widetilde{\varrho}^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Next, we notice that
Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})\subset Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})
, since
\widetilde{\varrho}<s
as well as
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}}\geq\lambda_{s}
. This allows us to increase the domain of integration from
Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})
to
Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})
. Applying subsequently the sub-intrinsic property d), we find that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\varrho}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\leq 2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\Big(\frac{s}{\widetilde{\varrho}}\Big)^{N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\frac{1}{|Q_{s}|}\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};s}^{(\lambda_{s})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{s^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq 4^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\Big(\frac{s}{\widetilde{\varrho}}\Big)^{N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\lambda_{s}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

In turn, we obtain

\widetilde{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\varrho}}\leq 4^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{s}{\widetilde{\varrho}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda_{s}.

Recalling that
\varrho\leq\widetilde{\varrho}\leq s
, we arrive at

\lambda_{\varrho}\leq 4^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{s}{\varrho}\Big)^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda_{s},

which is (
5.5
).
This proves the first assertion in b). The second one follows directly from (
5.4
) together with
\lambda_{R}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{R}
.

Step 6. Proof of e).

If
\widetilde{\varrho}\in(\frac{R}{2},R]
, then by b) with
s=R
,

\lambda_{\varrho}=\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}}\leq 4^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{R}{\widetilde{\varrho}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\lambda_{R}.

Invoking (
5.4
) gives

\lambda_{\varrho}\leq 4^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}4^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\lambda_{o}\leq 4^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+3{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\lambda_{o}.

If instead
\widetilde{\varrho}\in[\varrho,\frac{R}{2}]
, then by definition
\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}}=\lambda_{\varrho}
,
and the cylinder
Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}}}(z_{o})
satisfies (
5.3
). This establishes e).
∎

5.2.
Intrinsic Vitali covering lemma

The next step is a Vitali–type covering property adapted to the intrinsic geometry encoded by
\varrho\mapsto\lambda_{z;\varrho}
. Given any family of cylinders
Q_{4\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(z)
as in Lemma
5.1
with small radii
\varrho<R/\hat{c}
, one can extract a countable subfamily of pairwise disjoint cylinders whose
\tfrac{\hat{c}}{4}
-enlargements cover the whole family, for a structural constant
\hat{c}=\hat{c}(N,p,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})\geq 20
. The selection follows the classical Vitali scheme but hinges on the monotonicity, scale control, and sub–intrinsic bounds from Lemma
5.1
, together with a quantitative inclusion of overlapping cylinders that compensates for the intrinsic anisotropy. This covering will be the backbone of the reverse Hölder improvement and higher–integrability arguments.

Lemma 5.2
.

There exists a constant
\hat{c}=\hat{c}(N,p,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})\geq 20
with the following property. If
\mathcal{F}
is any collection of cylinders
Q_{4\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(z)
, where each
Q_{\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(z)
is a cylinder as in Lemma
5.1
with radius
\varrho\in\bigl(0,\tfrac{R}{\hat{c}}\bigr)
, then there exists a countable subfamily
\mathcal{G}\subset\mathcal{F}
of pairwise disjoint cylinders such that

(5.6)

\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}}Q\subseteq\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}\widehat{Q},

where
\widehat{Q}
denotes the
\tfrac{\hat{c}}{4}
-fold enlargement of
Q
(same center and parameter
\lambda
): if
Q=Q_{4\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(z)
, then
\widehat{Q}=Q_{\hat{c}\,\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(z)
.

Proof..

Fix
\hat{c}\geq 20
. For
j\in\mathbb{N}
subdivide
\mathcal{F}
into

\mathcal{F}_{j}:=\Big\{\,Q_{4\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(z)\in\mathcal{F}:\tfrac{R}{2^{j}\hat{c}}<\varrho\leq\tfrac{R}{2^{j-1}\hat{c}}\,\Big\}.

Choose
\mathcal{G}_{1}\subset\mathcal{F}_{1}
as an arbitrary maximal subfamily of pairwise disjoint cylinders. By Lemma
5.1
b) and the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
, each element of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
has a uniform lower volume bound; hence
\mathcal{G}_{1}
is finite.
Assume that
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2},\dots,\mathcal{G}_{k-1}
have been constructed for some integer
k\geq 2
. Define
\mathcal{G}_{k}
to be any maximal subfamily of
\mathcal{F}_{k}
consisting of cylinders that are pairwise disjoint and disjoint from all previously selected cylinders:

\mathcal{G}_{k}\subset\Big\{\,Q\in\mathcal{F}_{k}:\ Q\cap Q^{\ast}=\varnothing\ \text{for every}\ Q^{\ast}\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathcal{G}_{j}\,\Big\}.

As in the case
k=1
, each element of
\mathcal{F}_{k}
enjoys a uniform lower bound on its volume (by Lemma
5.1
b) and the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{k}
), hence
\mathcal{G}_{k}
is finite. Consequently,

\mathcal{G}:=\bigcup_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{G}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{F}

is a countable family of pairwise disjoint cylinders. It remains to show that for every

Q\in\mathcal{F}
there exists

Q^{\ast}\in\mathcal{G}
with
Q\subset\widehat{Q}^{\ast}
.
To this end, fix a cylinder

Q=Q_{4\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(z)\in\mathcal{F}
, and choose

j\in\mathbb{N}
with
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j}
.
If
Q\in\mathcal{G}_{j}
, then
Q\subset\widehat{Q}
is trivial.
Suppose
Q\not\in\mathcal{G}_{j}
.
By maximality of the families
\mathcal{G}_{i}

up to level
j
, there exists a cylinder

Q^{\ast}=Q_{4\varrho_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(z_{\ast})\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{j}\mathcal{G}_{i}\quad\mbox{with}\quad Q\cap Q^{\ast}\not=\varnothing.

In the sequel we show that
Q\subset\widehat{Q}^{\ast}
.
First, we observe that the properties
\varrho\leq\tfrac{R}{2^{j-1}\hat{c}}

and
\varrho_{\ast}>\tfrac{R}{2^{j}\hat{c}}
imply
\varrho\leq 2\varrho_{\ast}
.
Since
Q\cap Q^{\ast}\neq\varnothing
,
\Lambda_{4\varrho}(t)\cap\Lambda_{4\varrho_{\ast}}(t_{\ast})\not=\varnothing
. Hence

|t-t_{\ast}|\leq 16\varrho^{2}+16\varrho_{\ast}^{2}.

For any
\tau\in\Lambda_{4\varrho}(t)
,

|\tau-t_{\ast}|\leq|\tau-t|+|t-t_{\ast}|\leq 16\varrho^{2}+(16\varrho^{2}+16\varrho_{\ast}^{2})=32\varrho^{2}+16\varrho_{\ast}^{2}\leq 144\,\varrho_{\ast}^{2},

where we used
\varrho\leq 2\varrho_{\ast}
. Since
\hat{c}\geq 20
, one has
144\,\varrho_{\ast}^{2}\leq(\hat{c}\varrho_{\ast})^{2}
, and therefore

\Lambda_{4\varrho}(t)\subseteq\Lambda_{\hat{c}\varrho_{\ast}}(t_{\ast})
, which ensures

\Lambda_{4\varrho}(t)\subseteq\Lambda_{\hat{c}\varrho_{\ast}}(t_{\ast})
since
\hat{c}\geq 20
.
To prove the corresponding spatial inclusion
B_{4\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(x)\subseteq B_{\hat{c}\,\varrho_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(x_{\ast})
, we first establish the comparison estimate

(5.7)

\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}\leq 72^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda_{z;\varrho}\,.

To this aim we use Lemma
5.1
e), which consists of two alternatives. In the first alternative we have
\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}\leq 4^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+3{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\lambda_{o}
, which in view of
\lambda_{z;\varrho}\geq\lambda_{o}
immediately implies (
5.7
). In the second alternative of Lemma
5.1
e) there exists a radius
\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}\in[\varrho_{\ast},R]
such that the cylinder
Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(z_{\ast})
is intrinsic, i.e.

(5.8)

\displaystyle\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=\frac{1}{|Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}|}\iint_{Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(z_{\ast})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{\ast},\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

We distinguish between the cases
\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}\leq\frac{R}{9}
and
\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}>\frac{R}{9}
. We begin with the latter. Using (
5.8
), Lemma
2.3
, the definitions of
\lambda_{o}
and
\lambda_{z;\varrho}\geq\lambda_{o}
, we estimate

\displaystyle\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\Big(\frac{4R}{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}\Big)^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\frac{|Q_{4R}|}{|Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}|}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{4R}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{(4R)^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\Big(\frac{4R}{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}\Big)^{N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}36^{N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\lambda_{z;\varrho}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}},

which can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}\leq 2^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}36^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda_{z;\varrho}\,.

This yields (
5.7
) in the present case.

It remains to treat the case
\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}\leq\frac{R}{9}
.
For that, we claim the inclusion

(5.9)

Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(z_{\ast})\subseteq Q_{9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z;9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}})}(z).

First, since
\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}\geq\varrho_{\ast}
and
|t-t_{\ast}|<(4\varrho)^{2}+(4\varrho_{\ast})^{2}\leq 80\,\varrho_{\ast}^{2}\leq 80\,\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}^{2}
, we have

\Lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}(t_{\ast})\subseteq\Lambda_{\mu\,\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}(t)
.
Moreover,

(5.10)

|x-x_{\ast}|\leq 4\lambda_{z;\varrho}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho+4\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho_{\ast}.

We may assume
\lambda_{z;\varrho}\leq\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}
, since the comparison estimate (
5.7
) is trivial otherwise. Using the monotonicity of
\varrho\mapsto\lambda_{z;\varrho}
and the chain
\varrho\leq 2\varrho_{\ast}\leq 2\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}\leq 9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}
, we obtain

\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}\geq\lambda_{z;\varrho}\geq\lambda_{z;9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}.

Combining the last two displays gives

\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}+|x-x_{\ast}|\leq 5\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}+4\lambda_{z;\varrho}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho\leq 9\lambda_{z;9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast},

and hence

B_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(x_{\ast})\subseteq B_{9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z;9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}})}(x).

Together with the time–interval inclusion this proves the cylinder inclusion (
5.9
).
Using (
5.8
), Lemma
2.3
, the inclusion (
5.9
), and Lemma
5.1
d) with

\varrho=9\varrho_{\ast}
and
s=9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}|}\iint_{Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(z_{\ast})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{\ast},\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{18^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{|Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}|}\iint_{Q_{9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z;9\varrho_{\ast}})}(z)}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z,9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z;9\varrho_{\ast}})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{(9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast})^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 36^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\frac{\big|Q_{9\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z;9\varrho_{\ast}})}\big|}{|Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}_{\ast}}|}\lambda_{z;9\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=36^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}9^{N+2}\lambda_{z;9\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

Hence

\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}\leq 36^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}9^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2)}\lambda_{z;9\varrho_{\ast}},

which in view of the inequality
\lambda_{z;9\varrho_{\ast}}\leq\lambda_{z;\varrho}
that follows from the monotonicity of
\varrho\mapsto\lambda_{z;\varrho}
proves (
5.7
) also in the remaining case.

The preliminary bound (
5.7
) will now allow us to show the inclusion

Q=Q_{4\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(z)\;\subseteq\;Q_{\hat{c}\,\varrho_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(z_{\ast})=\widehat{Q}^{\ast},

which yields (
5.6
). The time–interval inclusion
\Lambda_{4\varrho}(t)\subseteq\Lambda_{\hat{c}\varrho_{\ast}}(t_{\ast})
has already been shown, so it suffices to treat the spatial part. From (
5.10
), (
5.7
), and
\varrho\leq 2\varrho_{\ast}
, we conclude

\displaystyle\lambda_{z;\varrho}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\,4\varrho+|x-x_{\ast}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\lambda_{z;\varrho}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\,4\varrho\;+\;\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\,4\varrho_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq 4\Big[\,4\cdot 72^{\frac{2-p}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}+1\,\Big]\,\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\,\varrho_{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\,\hat{c}\,\varrho_{\ast},

for a structural constant
\hat{c}=\hat{c}(N,p,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
. Hence

B_{4\varrho}^{(\lambda_{z;\varrho})}(x)\;\subseteq\;B_{\hat{c}\varrho_{\ast}}^{(\lambda_{z_{\ast};\varrho_{\ast}})}(x_{\ast}),

and together with the time inclusion we obtain
Q\subset\widehat{Q}^{\ast}
. This proves (
5.6
) and completes the argument.
∎

5.3.
Selection of critical intrinsic radii
