We localize the super-level sets of
|Du|
by a stopping–time selection on the intrinsic scales
\lambda_{z_{o};s}
: for a fixed threshold
\lambda>\lambda_{o}
, each Lebesgue point
z_{o}
corresponds to a maximal radius at which the normalized energy first reaches
\lambda^{p}
. The Vitali covering of these critical cylinders furnishes the geometric backbone for the ensuing good–
\lambda
estimate and higher–integrability.

Fix
R>0
and set

\displaystyle\lambda_{o}

\displaystyle:=1+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}\frac{|u-(u)_{4R}|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{(4R)^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}

(5.11)

\displaystyle\phantom{:=\,1\,}+\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}.

For
\lambda>\lambda_{o}
and
\varrho\in(0,2R]
define the
super-level set
of
|Du|
by

{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(\varrho,\lambda):=\Big\{z\in Q_{\varrho}:\mbox{$z$ is a Lebesgue point of $|Du|$ and
$|Du|(z)>\lambda$}\Big\}.

Lebesgue points here are understood with respect to the intrinsic cylinders constructed in Lemma
5.1
. By the Vitali–type covering Lemma
5.2
and the standard differentiation theorem (see, e.g.,
[
7
, 2.9.1]
),
\mathcal{L}^{N+1}
-a.e. point is a Lebesgue point with respect to this family.
Fix radii
R\leq R_{1}<R_{2}\leq 2R
. For any
z_{o}\in Q_{R_{1}}
,
\kappa\geq 1
, and
\varrho\in(0,\,R_{2}-R_{1}]
we have

Q_{\varrho}^{(\kappa)}(z_{o})\subseteq Q_{R_{2}}\subseteq Q_{2R}.

In what follows the level parameter
\lambda
will be restricted by

(5.12)

\lambda>\lambda_{o}^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}},\quad\mbox{where}\quad{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}:=\Big(\frac{32\hat{c}R}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}(N+p)}{p\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}>1,

where
\hat{c}=\hat{c}(N,p,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
is the structural constant from the Vitali–type covering Lemma
5.2
.
Fix
z_{o}\in{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
and, for
s\in(0,R]
, abbreviate
\lambda_{s}:=\lambda_{z_{o};s}
as in Lemma
5.1
. By the definition of
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
(and the Lebesgue differentiation property with respect to intrinsic cylinders),

\displaystyle\liminf_{s\downarrow 0}

\displaystyle\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{s}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg]

(5.13)

\displaystyle\geq|Du|^{p}(z_{o})>\lambda^{p}.

Fix
s
with

(5.14)

\displaystyle\frac{R_{2}-R_{1}}{\hat{c}}\leq s\leq R.

By the definition of
\lambda_{o}
, Lemma
5.1
b), and assumption (
5.14
), we estimate

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{s}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{|Q_{4R}|}{|Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}|}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\frac{|Q_{4R}|}{|Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}|}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{|Q_{4R}|}{|Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}|}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{|Q_{4R}|}{|Q_{s}|}\,\lambda_{s}^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\leq\Big(\frac{4R}{s}\Big)^{N+2}\,\lambda_{s}^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Big(\frac{4R}{s}\Big)^{N+2}\bigg[4^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{R}{s}\Big)^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}4^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\lambda_{o}\bigg]^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad=4^{\frac{4{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}N(2-p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{4R}{s}\Big)^{N+2+(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})\frac{N(2-p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}+\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad=8^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}N(2-p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{4R}{s}\Big)^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}(N+p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}

(5.15)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Big(\frac{32\hat{c}R}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}(N+p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=\big({\bm{\mathsf{B}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\big)^{p}<\lambda^{p},

where we also used the algebraic identity

\displaystyle N+2+(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})\frac{N(2-p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\big[(N+2)\big(N(p-2)+p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\big)+N(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})(2-p)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\big[(N+2)p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}+N{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}(2-p)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}(N+p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}},

and the choice of
{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}
in (
5.12
).
By continuity of
s\mapsto\lambda_{s}
and absolute continuity of the integral, the left–hand side of (
5.3
) depends continuously on
s
. Hence, combining (
5.3
) and (
5.3
), there exists a maximal radius
0<\varrho_{z_{o}}<\tfrac{R_{2}-R_{1}}{\hat{c}}
at which equality is attained, i.e.

(5.16)

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho_{z_{o}}\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}=\lambda^{p}.

By the maximality of
\varrho_{z_{o}}
, it follows that for every
s\in(\varrho_{z_{o}},R]
,

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{s}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{s}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}s\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}<\lambda^{p}.

By the monotonicity of
s\mapsto\lambda_{s}

and the scale control in Lemma
5.1
b), we obtain for all
\varrho_{z_{o}}\leq s<\sigma\leq R
that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\sigma}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}

\displaystyle|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\sigma}^{(\lambda_{s})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{s}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\sigma\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda_{\sigma}}\Big)^{\frac{N(2-p)}{2}}\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\sigma}^{(\lambda_{\sigma})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\sigma}^{(\lambda_{\sigma})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{\sigma}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\sigma\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg]

(5.17)

\displaystyle<4^{\frac{N(2-p){\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Big(\frac{\sigma}{s}\Big)^{\frac{N(2-p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda^{p}.

Finally, recall that the cylinders were chosen so that their
\tfrac{\hat{c}}{4}
-enlargements remain inside
Q_{R_{2}}
; in particular,

Q_{\hat{c}\,\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\subseteq Q_{\hat{c}\,\varrho_{z_{o}}}(z_{o})\subseteq Q_{R_{2}}.

5.4.
Reverse Hölder inequality on intrinsic cylinders

A reverse Hölder estimate is now obtained at the intrinsic stopping scale. For
z_{o}\in{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
with stopping radius
\varrho_{z_{o}}
and parameter
\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}
, the sub– and super–intrinsic couplings verified above
allow us to invoke Proposition
4.8
on
Q_{2\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})
. This yields a reverse Hölder inequality for
Du
on intrinsic cylinders, which will serve as the input for Gehring’s self–improvement and the ensuing higher integrability.

Fix
\lambda
as in (
5.12
) and let
z_{o}\in{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
.
Denote by
\varrho_{z_{o}}
the stopping radius from (
5.16
), and set
\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}:=\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho_{z_{o}}}

(cf. Lemma
5.1
).
Our aim is to establish a reverse Hölder inequality for
Du
on the intrinsic cylinder
Q_{2\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})
. To verify the assumptions (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) of Proposition
4.8
, we first observe that Lemma
5.1
d) with
s=2\varrho_{z_{o}}
implies

\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};2\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{(2\varrho_{z_{o}})^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq 2^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}.

Thus, the sub–intrinsic coupling (
4.5
) holds on
Q_{2\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})
with
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}=2^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}
.

To verify (
4.6
), we first claim that
\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}
is bounded in terms of
\lambda
.
Indeed, by Lemma
5.1
e), either an explicit upper bound holds:

\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}\leq 4^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+3{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\lambda_{o}\leq{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}<\lambda,

where in the second inequality we used the definition of the constant
{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}
from (
5.12
). Alternatively, there exists a radius
\widetilde{\varrho}\in\big[\varrho_{z_{o}},\tfrac{R}{2}\big]
such that

\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{\frac{p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})}\frac{\big|u-(u)_{z_{o};\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}\big|^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}{\widetilde{\varrho}^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

The latter case also yields a bound similar to the former case in view of Lemma
4.7
applied on
Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})
. In fact, the sub–intrinsic condition (
4.3
) holds with
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}=2^{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}
by Lemma
5.1
d), while the super–intrinsic identity (
4.4
) with
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}=1
follows from the preceding display. Hence, Lemma
4.7
gives

\displaystyle\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{p}=\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\widetilde{\varrho}}^{(\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{\widetilde{\varrho}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\widetilde{\varrho}\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Moreover, using (
5.3
) with
s=\widetilde{\varrho}
and
\sigma=2\widetilde{\varrho}\in(s,R]
, we obtain

\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{p}\leq C2^{N+2+\frac{N(2-p)}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}(N+2+3{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})}\,\lambda^{p}.

In either alternative we thus have
\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}\leq C\lambda
as claimed.
Combining this with (
5.16
) yields

\displaystyle\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{p}\leq C\lambda^{p}

\displaystyle=C\Bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho_{z_{o}}\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg].

Since enlarging the cylinders on the right to
Q_{2\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})
only increases the integrals, the super–intrinsic condition (
4.6
) holds on

Q_{2\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}
with a constant
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\equiv{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
.
In any case, all hypotheses of Proposition
4.8
are met. Hence the proposition yields the reverse Hölder estimate

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\theta}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho_{z_{o}}{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\theta}}

(5.18)

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}},

where
\theta=\frac{Np}{N+2}<1

and
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
.

5.5.
Weak-type estimate for the super-level sets of
|Du|

So far, for every
\lambda
as in (
5.12
) and every
z_{o}\in{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
, we have constructed a cylinder
Q_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})
with
Q_{\hat{c}\,\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\subseteq Q_{R_{2}}
that satisfies the stopping time identity (
5.16
), the strict decay for larger radii (
5.3
), and the reverse Hölder estimate (
5.4
). These ingredients permit a Vitali-type covering argument yielding a reverse Hölder (weak-type) bound for the distribution function of
|Du|^{p}
. The argument proceeds as follows.
We define the
super-level set of the source terms
by

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(\varrho,\lambda)

\displaystyle\quad:=\Big\{z\in Q_{\varrho}:\mbox{$z$ is a Lebesgue point of $|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{\frac{1}{p-1}}$ and
$\big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\big](z)>\lambda$}\Big\}.

As before, Lebesgue points are understood with respect to the intrinsic cylinders constructed in §
5.1
; by Lemma
5.2
and the differentiation theorem,
\mathcal{L}^{N+1}
-a.e. point of
Q_{\varrho}
is such a Lebesgue point.
Applying (
5.16
) and (
5.4
) we obtain, for every
z_{o}\in{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
and every
\eta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+\big|\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho_{z_{o}}\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}\big|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{p}{\theta}}+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\eta^{p}\lambda^{p}+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}^{\frac{p}{\theta}}+C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}^{\frac{1}{q}},

for some
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
, where we again abbreviated
\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}}=\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho_{z_{o}}}
and

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\big|Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\big|}\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\cap{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\big|Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\big|}\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\cap{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Moreover, to get the last estimate, we decomposed the averages over
Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}
according to the sets
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)
and
Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}\setminus{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)
, and analogously for
{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}
on the source terms; the sub-level contribution is bounded by
(\eta\lambda)^{p}
and can be absorbed after choosing
\eta>0
small enough.

In order to control
{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}
, we apply Hölder’s inequality and employ (
5.3
) with
s=\varrho_{z_{o}}
and
\sigma=4\varrho_{z_{o}}
. This yields

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}^{\frac{p}{\theta}-1}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{1-\frac{\theta}{p}}\leq C\,\lambda^{p-\theta}.

For
{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}
, we use Young’s inequality with conjugate exponents
q^{\prime}=\frac{q}{q-1}
and
q
. This gives

\displaystyle C{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{4}\lambda^{p}+\frac{C\,\lambda^{-p(q-1)}}{\big|Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\big|}\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\cap{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Choose and fix a parameter

(5.19)

\eta=\eta(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})\in(0,1)\quad\text{such that}\quad\eta^{p}=\frac{1}{4C},

where
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
is the constant from the previous estimate. With this choice, the
\lambda^{p}
-terms are absorbed into the left-hand side. Multiplying the resulting inequality by
\big|Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\big|
yields

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}\big|Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\cap{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\lambda^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+C\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\cap{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\lambda^{-p(q-1)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

with
C=C(N,m,\nu,L,p,r)
. In particular,
\eta
is fixed once and for all in terms of the structural parameters only, independent of
z_{o}
,
\lambda
, and
R_{1},R_{2}
.

Applying (
5.3
) with
s=\varrho_{z_{o}}
and
\sigma=\hat{c}\,\varrho_{z_{o}}
, and recalling that
\hat{c}=\hat{c}(N,p,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}\geq\frac{1}{C}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{\hat{c}\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Combining the preceding bounds, we obtain

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{\hat{c}\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\cap{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\!\!\lambda^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(5.20)

\displaystyle\quad+C\iint_{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o})\cap{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\!\!\lambda^{-p(q-1)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

for a constant
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
.
Since (
5.5
) holds for every center
z_{o}\in{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
,
the super-level set
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
can be covered by the family of cylinders

\mathcal{F}:=\Big\{Q_{4\varrho_{z_{o}}}^{(\lambda_{z_{o};\varrho_{z_{o}}})}(z_{o}):\ z_{o}\in{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)\Big\},

whose elements are contained in
Q_{R_{2}}
and on each of which (
5.5
) is valid.
By the Vitali-type covering Lemma
5.2
, there exists a countable disjoint subfamily

\Big\{\,Q_{4\varrho_{z_{i}}}^{(\lambda_{z_{i};\varrho_{z_{i}}})}(z_{i})\,\Big\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{F}

with

{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}Q_{\hat{c}\,\varrho_{z_{i}}}^{(\lambda_{z_{i};\varrho_{z_{i}}})}(z_{i})\subseteq Q_{R_{2}}.

Applying (
5.5
) to each cylinder
Q_{4\varrho_{z_{i}}}^{(\lambda_{z_{i};\varrho_{z_{i}}})}(z_{i})
and summing over the disjoint subfamily yields

\displaystyle\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\lambda^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+C\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\lambda^{-p(q-1)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

with
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
.
To balance the levels, consider the intermediate layer
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\eta\lambda)\setminus{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)
. Since
R_{1}<R_{2}
, one has
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\eta\lambda)\subset{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)
, and on this layer
|Du|\leq\lambda
. Hence

\displaystyle\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\eta\lambda)\setminus{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\eta\lambda)}\lambda^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Adding the two preceding bounds and then replacing
\eta\lambda
by
\lambda
(recalling that
\eta\in(0,1)
is fixed in terms of
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
only) yields: for every
\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}:=\eta{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{1},\lambda)}

\displaystyle|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

(5.21)

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(R_{2},\lambda)}\!\!\lambda^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\lambda)}\!\!\lambda^{-p(q-1)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
and
\theta=\tfrac{Np}{N+2}
. This is the desired reverse Hölder–type inequality for the distribution function of
Du
.

5.6.
Proof of Theorem
1.3

Having established the
reverse Hölder–type level–set inequality
(
5.5
) for
|Du|^{p}
in the previous subsection, we now turn to its quantitative consequences.
The next step is to convert this level–set estimate into an integral gain.
To this end, we multiply (
5.5
) by
\lambda^{p\varepsilon-1}
and integrate with respect to
\lambda
; using Fubini’s theorem together with Hölder’s and Young’s inequality to handle the source terms, and finally applying the iteration Lemma
2.1
, we infer that
|Du|\in L^{p(1+\varepsilon)}(Q_{R})
.
The lower bound for
\lambda_{o}
in (
5.3
) fixes the constants and yields the stated quantitative estimate.

To proceed with the next step of the argument,
fix
{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}>\lambda_{1}=\eta{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}
and define the
truncation

|Du|_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}:=\min\{|Du|,{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}\},

and, for
\varrho\in(0,2R]
, the corresponding super-level set

{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}(\varrho,\lambda):=\big\{z\in Q_{\varrho}\colon\ |Du|_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}(z)>\lambda\big\}.

Note that
|Du|_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}\leq|Du|
a.e., that
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}(\varrho,\lambda)=\varnothing
if
{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}\leq\lambda
, and that
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}(\varrho,\lambda)={\bm{\mathsf{E}}}(\varrho,\lambda)
if
{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}>\lambda
. Hence, by (
5.5
), for every
{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}>\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda)}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{2},\lambda)}\lambda^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\lambda)}\lambda^{-p(q-1)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
and
\theta=\tfrac{Np}{N+2}
.
Since
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}(\varrho,\lambda)=\varnothing
for
{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}\leq\lambda
, the preceding estimate holds for all
{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}>0
. Multiplying by
\lambda^{p\varepsilon-1}
with
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
(to be fixed below) and integrating over
(\lambda_{1},\infty)
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{\infty}\lambda^{p\varepsilon-1}

\displaystyle\bigg[\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda)}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{\infty}\lambda^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta-1}\bigg[\iint_{\bm{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{2},\lambda)}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{\infty}\lambda^{p(1-q+\varepsilon)-1}\bigg[\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\lambda)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\mathrm{d}\lambda.

Applying Fubini’s theorem and reordering the integrations gives, for the left–hand side,

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\lambda^{p\varepsilon-1}\bigg[\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda)}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle=\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda_{1})}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\bigg[\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}\lambda^{p\varepsilon-1}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{p\varepsilon}\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda_{1})}\Big[|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}-\lambda_{1}^{p\varepsilon}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

For the first term on the right–hand side, since
\theta<p
,

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\lambda^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta-1}\bigg[\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{2},\lambda)}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle=\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{2},\lambda_{1})}|Du|^{\theta}\bigg[\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}\lambda^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta-1}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{2},\lambda_{1})}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{p-\theta}\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{2},\lambda_{1})}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Note that

1-q+\varepsilon<-\tfrac{N}{p}+\varepsilon\leq-\tfrac{N+2}{2}+\varepsilon<-\tfrac{N}{2}<0

because
q>\tfrac{N+p}{p}
and
p\leq\tfrac{2N}{N+2}
. Then, Fubini’s theorem gives

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\lambda^{p(1-q+\varepsilon)-1}\bigg[\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\lambda)}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle=\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\lambda_{1})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\bigg[\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}}\lambda^{p(1-q+\varepsilon)-1}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\lambda_{1})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\bigg[\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{\infty}\lambda^{p(1-q+\varepsilon)-1}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{1}^{-p(q-1-\varepsilon)}}{p(q-1-\varepsilon)}\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{G}}}(R_{2},\lambda_{1})}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{1}^{-p(q-1-\varepsilon)}}{p}\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Inserting the preceding bounds and multiplying by
p\varepsilon
yields

\displaystyle\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda_{1})}

\displaystyle|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{1}^{p\varepsilon}\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda_{1})}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+\frac{C\,\varepsilon}{p-\theta}\iint_{{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{2},\lambda_{1})}|Du|_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\,}+\frac{C\,\varepsilon}{\lambda_{1}^{p(q-1-\varepsilon)}}\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

with
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
and
\theta=\tfrac{Np}{N+2}
.
On the complement
Q_{R_{1}}\setminus{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}(R_{1},\lambda_{1})
one has
|Du|_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}\leq\lambda_{1}
, hence

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R_{1}}\setminus{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda_{1})}

\displaystyle|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{1}^{p\varepsilon}\iint_{Q_{R_{1}}\setminus{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}(R_{1},\lambda_{1})}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Adding this to the inequality over
{\bm{\mathsf{E}}}_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}(R_{1},\lambda_{1})
and using
|Du|_{{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}}\leq|Du|
yields

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R_{1}}}

\displaystyle|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\ast}\varepsilon}{p-\theta}\iint_{Q_{R_{2}}}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\lambda_{1}^{p\varepsilon}\iint_{Q_{2R}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq\ }+\frac{C\,\varepsilon}{\lambda_{1}^{p(q-1-\varepsilon)}}\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where
C_{\ast}=C_{\ast}(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})\geq 1
.
Choose

0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{o},\qquad\varepsilon_{o}:=\frac{p-\theta}{2C_{\ast}}<\tfrac{1}{2},

where
\varepsilon_{o}
depends only on
N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
.
Since
\lambda_{1}=\eta{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}\lambda_{o}^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}
with
{\bm{\mathsf{B}}},\,\lambda_{o}\geq 1
and
\eta\in(0,1]

from (
5.19
),
we have

\lambda_{1}^{\,p\varepsilon}\leq{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}^{\,p\varepsilon}\lambda_{o}^{\,p\varepsilon\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\leq{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}^{p}\,\lambda_{o}^{\,p\varepsilon\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}},\qquad\lambda_{1}^{\,p(q-1-\varepsilon)}\geq\eta^{\,p(q-1)}\lambda_{o}^{\,p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}(q-1-\varepsilon)}.

Inserting these bounds into the preceding estimate and absorbing the
\eta,{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}
-factors into the constant (recalling the definition of
{\bm{\mathsf{B}}}
in (
5.12
)) yields: for any radii
R\leq R_{1}<R_{2}\leq 2R
,

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R_{1}}}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{Q_{R_{2}}}|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\,\Big(\frac{R}{R_{2}{-}R_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{2{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}(N+p)}{{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}}\lambda_{o}^{p\varepsilon\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\iint_{Q_{2R}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C}{\lambda_{o}^{p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}(q-1-\varepsilon)}}\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

where
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
, and
\bm{\lambda}_{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}=N(p-2)+p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}
.
Applying the iteration Lemma
2.1
to the preceding inequality yields

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{R}}

\displaystyle|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}^{p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta}|Du|^{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\,\lambda_{o}^{p\varepsilon\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\iint_{Q_{2R}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\frac{C}{\lambda_{o}^{p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}(q-1-\varepsilon)}}\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

with
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
.
Since the right–hand side of the inequality does not depend on
{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}
,
and the integrability exponent of
|Du|_{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}
satisfies
p(1+\varepsilon)-\theta>0
,
we may let
{\bm{\mathsf{k}}}\uparrow\infty
and apply the monotone convergence theorem
to obtain the higher integrability estimate

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}}

\displaystyle|Du|^{p(1+\varepsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\,\lambda_{o}^{p\varepsilon\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\frac{C}{\lambda_{o}^{p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}(q-1-\varepsilon)}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Invoking the definition from (
5.3
),

\lambda_{o}\;\geq\;\max\big\{1,\ {\bm{\mathsf{I}}}^{\,\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{q\bm{\lambda}_{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}}\big\},\qquad{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}:=\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

we estimate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda_{o}^{p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}(q-1-\varepsilon)}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}}

\displaystyle\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\lambda_{o}^{p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}(q-1-\varepsilon)}}{\bm{\mathsf{I}}}^{\frac{q-1-\varepsilon}{q}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\lambda_{o}^{(q-1-\varepsilon)(p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}-\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}})}}\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{q}}.

To obtain the last line, we used that

p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}-\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}=p{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\Big)+\frac{N(2-p)}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}>0,

and that
\lambda_{o}\geq 1
.
Consequently, we have

\lambda_{o}^{(q-1-\varepsilon)(p\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}-\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}})}\geq 1
.
Substituting the foregoing bound for the source terms contribution
into the higher–integrability estimate, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{R}}

\displaystyle|Du|^{p(1+\varepsilon)}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda_{o}^{p\varepsilon\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{2}}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}}|Du|^{p}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+C\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{2R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R\,{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{q}},

with
C=C(N,p,C_{o},C_{1},q,{\bm{\mathsf{r}}})
.
On the other hand, a direct application of the zero–order energy inequality
(
4.1
) on
Q_{4R}
with
a=(u)_{8R}
yields

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{4R}}|Du|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\Bigg[1+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{8R}}\frac{|u-(u)_{8R}|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(8R)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{8R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{q\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Bigg],

and consequently,

\lambda_{o}\leq C\Bigg[1+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{8R}}\frac{|u-(u)_{8R}|^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{(8R)^{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{2}{\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}+\bigg[\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-\!-$}}\kern-8.47218pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-5.86113pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-4.56949pt}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\!-$}}\kern-3.81949pt}\!\iint_{Q_{8R}}\Big[|{\bm{\mathsf{F}}}|^{qp}+|R{\bm{\mathsf{f}}}|^{qp^{\prime}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{\frac{{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}{q\bm{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathsf{r}}}}}\Bigg].

This completes the proof of Theorem
1.3
.
∎
